De Laurentiis. “Nessun caso, ho preferito bloccare la conferenza. Giochiamo ogni tre giorni”

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Aurelio De Laurentiis, presidente del Napoli, scrive su X in merito all’annullamento della conferenza stampa: “In casa Napoli non esiste nessun caso. Quando mi sono accorto che stavamo per fare l’ennesima conferenza ho preferito bloccarla. Perché? Perché non credo che le conferenze stampa servano prima delle partite, considerando che giochiamo ogni tre giorni. Siamo in ritiro e bisogna evitare qualunque distrazione per i calciatori dalla loro concentrazione alla partita. Quando si va in ritiro la tutela di tutti deve essere totale. Aurelio De Laurentiis”.

A Former 1900s Lakeside Resort in Georgia Is for Sale as an $8.85 Million Private Retreat

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Owning waterfront property is one thing. Owning the entire lake is another.

The lake, created from a dam on Ichawaynochaway Creek, is just part of the 1,442-acre property in Leary, GA listed for $8.85 million.

"When you're on the water, it's about 250 acres of private water in this famous mill pond. You own the whole [thing]. You own everything," explains listing agent Jon Kohler with Jon Kohler Associates.

“When you're out in a boat, you're not used to being the only one in this enormous lake. It probably takes five or six hours to go around the whole thing. It’s huge.”

“It's just fascinating. There's really nothing else like it, nor can there ever be again,” Kohler says. “You control it. You're the only boat that's able to duck hunt, fish, there.”

Cordray's Mill was once a resort property with a dance hall, swimming lake, and more, dating back to the early 1900s and continuing at least through the 1960s.

“There are a couple of remnant guest homes from the era when it was a resort, it was one of the first resorts in Georgia,” he says. “People would come from all over for events and whatnot.”

One structure is a converted church that is now a four bedroom and 1.5 bathroom house, in addition other living spaces.

“There's a house back where the mill pond house used to stand. There's a house on the lake, but back during its heyday, there were all types of structures, stores, shops, everything,” Kohler says.

The two houses have a total of six bedrooms and two bathrooms.

Another structure is a converted grain silo with one bedroom and a bathroom.

“It's one of those great ideas that not many people are able to pull off because the walls are rounded. It’s a really quaint little home.”

But the draw isn’t all of the places for people to stay, it’s about the activities.

“My favorite thing is the diversity of the habitats. You have almost every great habitat that Southwest Georgia has, but on one property,” Kohler explains, adding there is irrigated farmland and timberland in addition to the lake and waterfront.

The trophy whitetail deer make the property something special.

“I see it being owned by somebody that wants something that no one else has, which is the diversity and really the trophy deer hunting and the trophy deer habitat,” he says.

“Just a wildness factor that most places don't offer. [The perfect buyer is] somebody that really just wants to have the best of the best trophy whitetail, a lot of turkeys, and top it off with waterfowl, in an abundance greater than what most places offer.”

The property could also meet another need, a social storm property, which is a term Kohler trademarked several years ago.

“We recognized a trend that people were buying land for the lack of better word in case s**t hit the fan,” he said with a laugh, comparing the term to a prepper's property.

“This property is an ideal social storm property as well as a legacy sporting estate. I see most of these great places, but I had no idea a place like that existed. It's like a unicorn.

"You have 250 acres of water backed up in one of the largest private lakes in southeast United States all to yourself.”

Tiffani Sherman is an experienced freelance writer based in the Tampa, Florida area who covers real estate, personal finance, travel, and more for a variety of print and online publications. She works to support her travel habit and has been to all seven continents and about 100 countries. Tiffani is a proud Duke Blue Devil.

"I didn't really like him" - James Trafford's first thoughts on new West Ham boss Scott Parker

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Leeds United goalkeeper James Trafford has revealed his initial relationship with new West Ham United boss Scott Parker was difficult during their time together at Burnley.

The duo were part of a record-breaking Burnley side, with the club accumulating 100 points and keeping a record 30 clean sheets in the second tier. They conceded just 16 goals as they were promoted alongside Leeds United.

Now languishing around the relegation zone in the Championship, James Trafford has joined Leeds, while West Ham United have Scott Parker in charge. It's all change across the three clubs, highlighting just how quickly things can change in football.

Trafford has revealed that, despite Burnley's excellent form with him in goal, Parker did not see eye-to-eye with the goalkeeper. The 24-year-old spoke to the BBC and revealed his thoughts.

He said: "Yeah, I had a big one with Scott Parker. We had a very, very weird start to my time at Burnley with him. And I don't think he liked me too much.

"I didn't really like him too much. It was just weird. We didn't like, we couldn't connect, it was hard. But then, by the end of it, he understood me really, really well. And we got along really, really well."

He was asked: "How did you get to that point?"

Trafford added: "I don't know. I actually don't know."

The interviewer replied: "It probably helped that you were playing pretty well on the pitch, didn't it?"

Trafford concluded: "Probably. Yeah, but he really understood me."

Trafford's quirkiness wasn't for everyone but some enjoyed his company at Burnley, including Bristol City's Scott Twine. He spoke of their friendship at Turf Moor, adding: "Well, I mean, I have Pokemon cards. Love Pokemon cards. Oh, I love them."

The interviewer asked: "What do you trade them?"

He said: "No, just open the pack. Cool. Good card. Put them in my binder. Collecting all the sets.

"I've always done it. I remember I used to live with Scott Twine and we were so bored in a pre-season and we did loads of different things.

"And one of them was just go buy them. Everything had to be a competition as well, so we ended up getting big into tennis. That was one thing.

"We used to play card games against each other on our phones and then the other thing was Pokemon cards. We used to just open them. It's just something I've done since."

On his relationship with Lawrence Vigoroux and how that influenced him at Turf Moor, Trafford also said: "Obviously, there's a lot of other goalies that I have watched and really like parts of their game, like Lawrence Vigoroux. I really enjoyed working with him. He had some really good things.

"Then, obviously, everyone talks about [Manuel] Neuer, [Thibaut] Courtois, and like the big hitters, but also some goalies that haven't

played at that level that are still [big influences]. You can take bits from their game."

Burnley supporters may be surprised to hear that Trafford and Parker did not immediately click, given how successful their partnership ultimately became at Turf Moor.

Trafford was outstanding behind a side that set Championship records during the 2024/25 promotion campaign, while Parker's organisation helped produce one of the most formidable defensive units the second tier has seen.

Will Burnley survive this season? Comment your thoughts below!

FLW have ranked the 10 best EFL Championship stadiums for atmosphere so far in the 2026/27 season.

The contrast with the current side could hardly be greater. Burnley have gone from conceding just 16 goals across 46 Championship matches in 2024/25 to shipping 16 in their opening seven games of this season.

Their defensive performances have been particularly poor compared with the standards Trafford and Parker helped establish, with a lack of organisation, consistency, and reliability at the back exposing just how far the club have fallen from that record-breaking team.

Tottenham to submit formal complaint after controversial red card decision at Leeds

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Tottenham, featuring team news and expert analysis from Sam Tabuteau

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Tottenham are set to submit a formal complaint about the decision not to send off Jayden Bogle for his challenge on Mathys Tel during their 1-0 defeat to Leeds.

Tel had to come off shortly before half time after Bogle landed with his studs up on the Frenchman’s left Achilles at Elland Road.

Despite initially trying to play on, the forward soon signalled to come off and was later spotted with crutches and a protective boot on.

Roberto De Zerbi said in his post-match press conference that while further medical checks will take place, the initial fear is that Tel has suffered “a bad injury” and is likely to spend a considerable amount of time on the sidelines.

Standard Sport understands Tottenham believe Bogle, who received no punishment for the challenge despite a four-minute VAR review, should have been sent off and will write to the refereeing body, Pro Ref, to seek an explanation as to why Bogle was not dismissed.

While De Zerbi was reluctant to blame the decision for Spurs’ performance, the north London side lost control of the game after Tel’s withdrawal, with Noah Okafor eventually breaking the deadlock in the 79th minute.

On the incident, the head coach said he had full confidence in VAR despite venting his frustrations on the touchline and receiving a yellow card, which will now see him serve a touchline ban when Spurs return to action at home to Ipswich.

“No, I have big confidence in the referee,” De Zerbi said when asked if he would have wanted referee John Brooks to go to the monitor to review Bogle’s challenge.

“Nothing changes if he goes to watch the clip or not. I think there was the VAR, and if they didn’t take the right decision, I don’t know. But we have confidence, we have to keep our eyes on the performance where we can improve.”

U17 : On Aytone puis on bétonne

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Vous avez pu suivre cette rencontre en direct sur notre forum grâce à nos envoyés spéciaux Stéphanois et MachuPicchu42 : les U17 ont battu leurs homologues clermontois sur le plus petit des scores cet après-midi à Aimé-Jacquet.

Lors d'une première période guère emballante, seul l'ailier gauche Naël Dani a mis en danger des Auvergnats un peu plus entreprenants mais bien muselés par la charnière centrale Naoufan Mnemoi - Lucas Dagbo.

Juste après l'heure de jeu, sur une bonne passe du numéro 8 Sabri Sercu, Isaak Saydi a fait une percée supersonique avant de délivrer une passe décisive à Gibril Aytone, entré en jeu dès la 32e minute à la place de Léo Fayolle.

Les Verts ont beaucoup souffert pour conserver leur court mais très précieux avantage. Ils ont heureusement pu compter sur la maladresse des visiteurs, qui ont eu en outre la malchance de trouver la barre transversale d'Andrea Laugier à la 87e minute.

La compo : Laugier - Fayolle (Aytone, 33'), Mnemoi, Dagbo, Bufferne - Diakité (Giangreco, 61'), Sercu, Thibaudat - Seydi, Kimpioka, Dani (Tiambo, 90').

La réaction de David Le Moal : "En première mi-temps, on a eu quelques sorties de balle intéressantes, mais par contre il y a eu un manque de coordination et de connexion entre tous les joueurs de l'équipe. Je trouve qu'on a manqué d'énergie aussi. La seconde mi-temps a été encore plus laborieuse. On n'a fait quasiment que défendre après la pause. On a eu quelques contres sur lesquels on a mal joué les coups. On a beaucoup subi même en étant bien en place derrière. C'est dommage qu'on ait concédé autant d'occasions alors qu'on avait décidé de fermer un peu le jeu. Le résultat est bon, on a gagné. Mais en ce qui concerne le contenu de ce match, il n'y a pas grand chose de positif à retenir. On a décroché une victoire sans prendre de but avec beaucoup de U16 qui ne jouaient pas à leut poste donc ça fait quand même quelques satisfactions."

FacileRent Molinari Napoli, presentata la stagione 2026/27: sport, territorio e nuove ambizioni

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

– Passione, ambizione e vicinanza al territorio. All’insegna di questi aspetti la Molinari Napoli ha presentato il nuovo anno sportivo ed in particolare la stagione della squadra che competerà in Serie B2 femminile. Un evento – quello tenutosi sabato 7 novembre al PalaVesuvio – che ha coinvolto società, istituzioni, partner, staff tecnico e atlete, per raccontare obiettivi, ambizioni e prospettive di un progetto che vuole continuare a investire nello sport e nel territorio.

«Lavoriamo sul territorio da anni, avvicinando i giovani di Ponticelli allo sport. Da cinque anni sentiamo la vicinanza dell’Amministrazione comunale che ci ha aiutato in qualsiasi circostanza», ha dichiarato il Presidente della Molinari Napoli, Gennaro Nappo. «Per la Serie B2 c’è tanta ambizione, voglia di crescere e di essere d’esempio per il settore giovanile. Abbiamo costruito una squadra competitiva e speriamo che il campo ce lo dimostri».

Presenti alla conferenza l’On. Borrelli e l’Ass. Ferrante

«Io non credo più all’idea del centro e delle periferie per come si sta evolvendo il sistema sociale del nostro territorio. Ogni luogo come questo (il PalaVesuvio di Via Argine n.d.r.) è oro colato da valorizzare», ha dichiarato l’Onorevole Francesco Emilio Borrelli, presente alla conferenza stampa. «Lo sport è qualcosa di straordinario non solo perché ci insegna la disciplina, il rispetto del prossimo e l’impegno, ma anche perché ci spinge a fare attività fisica. Noi siamo in questo momento la Regione più obesa d’Italia per quanto riguarda i bambini. Bambini che non fanno sport: è importante fargli fare un’attività pulita e sana». Borrelli ha concluso il suo intervento sottolineando la vicinanza alla squadra: «Verrò sicuramente a vedere una delle vostre partite: tiferò per voi».

«Lo sport è una leva fondamentale, anche se a volte viene considerato secondario, soprattutto per quanto riguarda i supporti economici», ha dichiarato l’Assessora allo Sport ed alle Pari Opportunità del Comune di Napoli, Emanuela Ferrante. «Cinque anni fa, con grande difficoltà, ho provato a recuperare delle somme per riqualificare gli impianti, senza i quali non si può andare da nessuna parte. A conclusione di questo anno, che ha visto Napoli essere Capitale Europea dello Sport, posso dire con fierezza e con grande gioia che abbiamo investito 35 milioni di euro su tanti gli impianti sportivi. Partendo dagli impianti riqualificati si può ambire a realizzare quello che penso sia il diritto di tutti i ragazzi: fare sport».

L’Assessora si è rivolta poi direttamente alla squadra. «Siete una testimonianza ed un esempio per tutte le ragazze ed i ragazzi di Ponticelli. Questa stagione sarà una straordinaria esperienza e cercheremo di far riempire la palestra ogni volta che giocherete in casa».

Buy IHG One Rewards Points With 100% Bonus (0.5 Cents Each): Worth It?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

IHG One Rewards is currently offering a limited time 100% bonus on purchased points. With this, IHG One Rewards members can purchase points for as little as 0.5 cents each. Is this a good deal?

While I wouldn’t recommend buying points without a specific use in mind, there are absolutely ways to get outsized value, and this is in line with the best offers that we see from the program. With the right strategy, this could be an opportunity to get a significant discount on an IHG hotel stay, and I’ll show exactly how.

Link: Buy IHG One Rewards points with this promotion with a 100% bonus

Through 11:59PM ET on Tuesday, November 10, 2026, IHG One Rewards is offering a bonus on purchased points. Points can be purchased in increments of 1,000 points, and the bonus is tiered, as follows:

Note that the purchase of IHG points doesn’t count toward elite status.

Ordinarily you can buy IHG One Rewards points for as little as one cent each (the pricing isn’t linear), before any discounts or bonuses. With that in mind, if you were eligible for a 100% bonus and purchased 200,000 IHG One Rewards points pre-bonus, you’d receive a total of 400,000 points at a cost of $2,000, which is a rate of 0.5 cents per IHG point.

While The Brand New IHG One Rewards Premier Select Credit Card (learn more), The New IHG One Rewards Premier Credit Card (learn more), The New IHG One Rewards Credit Card (learn more), and The New IHG One Rewards Business Credit Card (learn more), offer a 30% discount on purchased points, that benefit only applies when buying points at the normal cost, and wouldn’t be applicable here.

IHG One Rewards ordinarily lets members purchase up to 150,000 points per calendar year, before any bonuses. However, during this promotion that cap has been increased to 200,000 points, pre-bonus.

When IHG One Rewards does offer a promotion on purchased points, the 100% bonus or 50% discount is the most common offer. A 100% bonus and 50% discount give you the same price per point, so you’ll typically pay 0.5 cents per point during a promotion.

Below is a chart with some of the recent promotions we saw from the program, before this one.

IHG One Rewards points purchases are processed by points.com, meaning they don’t count as a hotel purchase for the purposes of credit card spending. That means if you pay with an IHG credit card, you wouldn’t earn any bonus rewards, so it’s not treated as if it’s IHG spending.

Therefore I’d recommend using a card on which you’re trying to reach a minimum spending requirement, or otherwise, a credit card that maximizes your return on everyday spending. See this post for more on which credit cards are best for buying points.

IHG One Rewards has dynamic award pricing, which means there’s no minimum or maximum number of points a hotel stay can cost. Rather, the cost of an award stay generally correlates somewhat to the cost of a revenue stay.

However, that doesn’t necessarily mean you’ll get the same value with all hotel stays. There are many situations where you can get outsized value by buying points and then redeeming them. Let me just give a few examples, using stays I pulled up for random dates (again, pricing is dynamic, so this won’t reflect what you see every day).

For example, the Six Senses Kyoto is bookable for 165,000 points per night, and you could purchase those points for $825.

That represents massive savings over the cash cost, which is $1,493.

On the other end of the spectrum, the Holiday Inn Express & Suites Tampa is bookable for 33,000 points per night, and you could purchase those points for $165.

That represents massive savings over the cash cost, which is $230.

However, this won’t always be a good deal. For example, take the Kimpton Tres Rios in Playa del Carmen is bookable for 104,000 points per night, and you could purchase those points for $520.

That’s not a good deal compared to the cash cost, which is $429, so there you’d be better off just paying cash directly.

Another thing to keep in mind is that select IHG co-branded credit cards offer free night awards, and those are worth up to a certain number of points. What’s great is that these free night awards can typically be topped off with points, so there could be merit to buying IHG points so that you have points with which to top off these certificates.

You’ll get the most value from your IHG points if you have The Brand New IHG One Rewards Premier Select Credit Card (learn more), The New IHG One Rewards Premier Credit Card (learn more), and The New IHG One Rewards Business Credit Card (learn more).

That’s because the cards offer a fourth night free benefit, meaning that you can book a four night award stay for the cost of three nights. This is essentially the equivalent of 25% off in increments of four nights, since you can use this benefit an unlimited number of times.

Just to use the Six Senses Kyoto example above, if you earned 495,000 points (worth $2,475 at 0.5 cents per point), that would be enough for four nights with the fourth night free. That represents an absolutely massive discount.

IHG has over 15 hotel brands, so there are lots of options for redeeming your IHG One Rewards points. IHG points can be redeemed at the following properties, among others:

Everyone will value points differently. Based on my latest miles & points valuations, I consider IHG points to be worth 0.5 cents each, and I tend to value points pretty conservatively. So the purchase price is around what I value these points.

For non-elite members, IHG One Rewards points expire after 12 months of inactivity. However, any sort of points earning or redemption activity would reset the expiration of your points. Furthermore, IHG One Rewards points don’t expire for elite members in the program.

Keep in mind that IHG elite status is quite easy to earn. Just for having The Brand New IHG One Rewards Premier Select Credit Card (learn more) or The New IHG One Rewards Business Credit Card (learn more), you receive Platinum status, without any sort of a spending requirement.

IHG One Rewards doesn’t have blackout dates on award nights, but the program does have capacity controls. What this means is that some number of standard rooms will be made available for award redemptions every night, but that doesn’t mean that all standard rooms are available for award redemptions.

IHG One Rewards is offering a sale on purchased points. Through November 10, 2026, IHG One Rewards is offering a promotion, and members seem to be eligible for a 100% bonus, which is an opportunity to buy points for 0.5 cents each.

There are instances where buying points can save you over 50% on the cost of a luxury hotel stay. I also think there’s value in having a “reserve” of IHG points, given the brand’s global footprint. You can get even more value out of buying IHG points in conjunction with a premium IHG credit card, so you can get a fourth night free on award redemptions.

Arsenal’s David Raya on track to break remarkable Premier League record

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Arsenal goalkeeper David Raya is on track to break the Premier League record for the most clean sheets ever in a Premier League campaign.

Some optimists had suggested that Arsenal might evolve to a more expansive style after getting the monkey off their back with the Premier League title last season.

Mikel Arteta’s Gunners were certainly entertaining in their 3-0 victory over Coventry City on the opening weekend, but they’ve failed to score more than two goals in any of their nine league outings since.

But the reigning champions look very well-placed to keep their Premier League crown after grinding out wins time and time again. Seven of their nine wins this season have come with a clean sheet, while three of the last four have been via a 1-0 scoreline.

With seven clean sheets from 10 outings, that’s a 70% clean sheet ratio for their goalkeeper. If Raya can maintain that and stay fit and available over the course of the full 38-game campaign, he’ll end the season on 26 clean sheets – which would break a remarkable record that’s stood for over two decades.

We’ve taken a closer look at the keepers who have kept the most clean sheets in a Premier League season.

Raya has already established himself among the Premier League’s elite goalkeepers, winning three consecutive Golden Gloves to equal the record a feat shared by Joe Hart and Pepe Reina.

The Spaniard also kept a club-record 19 clean sheets for Arsenal during last season’s title-winning campaign, but he’s yet to make the top echelons of this list with 20+ clean sheets in one season.

Alisson, Ederson, Pepe Reina and Peter Schmeichel are the Premier League greats to have reached 20 clean sheets in a single Premier League season.

Ederson managed it twice, in 2018-19 and 2021-22, while Reina also appears twice thanks to his exploits at Liverpool in 2005-06 and 2008-09.

Alisson’s 20 came in 2021-22, while Schmeichel achieved the same number for Manchester United in 1994-95.

Reina had won three consecutive Golden Gloves between 2006 and 2008, but was denied a fourth when Edwin van der Sar claimed the prize in 2008-09.

It was a particularly satisfying season for the veteran Dutchman, who also helped Manchester United pip Rafael Benitez’s Liverpool to the Premier League title.

United had Cristiano Ronaldo, Wayne Rooney and Carlos Tevez at the peak of their powers, but their defensive foundations were arguably even more important. Rio Ferdinand and Nemanja Vidic formed a formidable partnership in front of Van der Sar, who was rarely required to do anything spectacular but was always there when called upon.

His remarkable run of 1,311 minutes without conceding, spanning 14 consecutive league matches, remains a Premier League record. We can’t see anyone – not even Arsenal and this miserly Gunners backline – ever breaking it.

Undoubtedly the greatest Premier League season ever produced by a team that didn’t win the title. Liverpool finished on 97 points, lost just once, conceded a league-best 22 goals and lifted the Champions League. And still finished second.

Alisson was instrumental in that remarkable campaign, keeping 21 clean sheets in his debut season at Anfield. Jurgen Klopp finally found the world-class goalkeeper he’d been missing, but even he couldn’t quite get them over the line in the title race.

We’ll be astonished if any team breaks the record set by Jose Mourinho’s imperial-era Chelsea in 2004-05. They conceded just 15 goals that season – even this Arsenal team is a third of the way there already.

But the clean sheet record might just be under threat if Arsenal can keep this up.

“We as a team took pride in getting clean sheets,” the legendary Czech goalkeeper reminisced when he was inducted into the Premier League Hall of Fame.

“It was not only the defenders and goalkeepers, it was everyone chipping in to make sure that if we can’t win, we don’t lose. If we don’t concede, you only need one shot and you get three points.

“That’s what I’m proud of. The clean sheets in that season were beautiful and something to remember, but if you finish third, then you’d think, ‘OK great, I have all the records, but all I really wanted was the title.

“Those clean sheets were the decisive factor in us winning the title. That’s what really stands out for me – that every clean sheet not only contributed to all the records, but especially to winning the title.”

You could apply those words to this Arsenal side, couldn’t you?

Godzilla Minus Zero Shuts Down One Of The Biggest Criticisms Of The Franchise

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

This article contains light spoilers for "Godzilla Minus Zero."

Godzilla has enjoyed a lasting popularity over his 70+ year existence, and it's no surprise. Ishirō Honda's 1954 "Godzilla" not only had a great title creature, but also involved a surprisingly weighty human drama that combined a love triangle with huge ethical conundrums, all of which fed into the film's impressively deep thematic material. It's a killer mixture, and Honda's movie is still thought of as one of the best movies ever made in the franchise all these years later. Although Toho Studios didn't immediately push the series towards bigger spectacle, the "Godzilla" films naturally took that course. Each successive film began creating bigger monster attack setpieces, and generated greater destruction by pitting the various monsters against each other.

This direction left any human characters with less screen time and importance in each movie. As such, the various writers and directors began to treat them more as perfunctory protagonists. While there have been several attempts to course correct this, it's a problem that persists. Even the MonsterVerse films have fallen prey to the common criticism that the human characters are second-rate.

This is part of why Takashi Yamazaki's "Godzilla Minus One" felt like such a breath of fresh air. That movie features a human story that is undeniably the core of the film, with Godzilla being more of a supporting feature. That approach continues in the sequel, "Godzilla Minus Zero," which doubles down on the great character work "Minus One" did by continuing the stories of already established characters, while also adding a few great new ones.

To be fair, there have been many prior movies in the "Godziilla" franchise that have featured some really compelling human characters and storylines to go with them. A few which leap to mind are "Godzilla vs. Biollante," "Godzilla vs. Destoroyah," and "Shin Godzilla." What these movies do well is find characters and a story that isn't completely based around attacks by Godzilla or other giant monsters, meaning that the characters are as proactive as they are reactive. When the latter becomes their primary function, the movies themselves essentially train the audience to not care about the humans as much, teaching them to wait patiently until the next big action scene occurs. In this way, the issues that some "Godzilla" movies have aren't all that different from a similar problem many genre movies face.

Where Takashi Yamazaki transcends this issue in "Godzilla Minus Zero" is in the way that he creates dramatic tension between the characters and Godzilla (or, in the film's case, Ghidorah) appearing. We the audience wish to see the giant monsters we came for, but Yamazaki's story positions many of these people as those who would likely be worse off should they turn up. Scientist Kenji Noda (Hidetaka Yoshioka) is barely keeping a still-hurting Japan's head above water post World War II, let alone having to deal with kaiju attacks. The continued existence of Ghidorah is a personal burden to meterologist Makoto Aoyama (Masami Nagasawa), while Godzilla's abilities present a dark temptation to professor Kanji Murakami (Min Tanaka). Meanwhile, Kōichi Shikishima (Ryunosuke Kamiki), who found his will to live in "Minus One," is struggling like mad to keep his family together. Yet in addition to caring for adopted daughter Akiko (Saw Nagatani), his wife Noriko (Minami Hamabe) has been infected with Godzillas cells, which may end up killing her or could be used in other ways. The tension between wanting to see Godzilla and not wanting these people to suffer as a result is what Yamazaki exploits so beautifully.

One of the most impressive aspects of "Godzilla Minus Zero" is its restraint. As a big-budget direct sequel to the very successful "Godzilla Minus One," it would've made sense for Takashi Yamazaki and his crew to go bigger with the film's visual effects. The temptation to do this must've been especially great considering that "Minus One" won the Academy Award for Best Visual Effects, making it the first "Godzilla" film ever to win an Oscar. Given that triumph, it's actually rather remarkable that Yamazaki didn't turn "Minus Zero" into a VFX extravaganza.

Instead, Yamazaki appears to deeply understand what makes visual effects great, which is their ability to enhance an already good story and its characters. The action sequences in "Minus Zero" are memorable because of how uniquely designed they are and how they utilize the characters. Moments like Shikishima flying through a village that's been upended by Ghidorah's gravity beam and Noda having to race against time to spring a trap on Ghidorah are made better because of the characters being directly involved in the action, not just observing.

All of this is why the big climax of "Godzilla Minus Zero" has relatively little to do with Godzilla and Ghidorah and is mostly to do with the fates of the human characters themselves. In a lesser movie, such scenes would feel like an obligatory denouement. in "Minus Zero," they're as riveting as anything else in the film. It may have taken over 70 years, but "Godzilla" has indeed finally circled back to where it began: a human drama that happens to feature a giant monster (charismatic as he may be).

"Godzilla Minus Zero" is in theaters now.

Serie A preview: Genoa vs. AC Milan – Team news, opposition insight, stats and prediction

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

A tough away trip awaits AC Milan on Sunday afternoon, hoping to restore some order against an in-form Genoa side.

What many hoped would be a positive week quickly turned into a negative one, as Milan failed to build on the derby win against Ferencvaros. In other words, they need to respond with a strong performance against Genoa, which is easier said than done.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Ruben Amorim didn’t have a press conference earlier today due to the tight schedule, so there are no new takeaways from him. Judging from the post-match interviews on Thursday, however, he’s expecting a lot more from his players at the Marassi.

Game date: Sunday, November 8 Kick-off time: 15:00 (CET) Venue: Stadio Luigi Ferraris Referee: Davide Massa (VAR: Camplone)

It’s been said for quite a few games now, but Amorim certainly isn’t being helped by the injury situation. In total, seven players will be on the sidelines for the game tomorrow, including key players like Samuel Chukwueze, Strahinja Pavlovic and Alexis Saelemaekers.

Milan were hoping to recover the Serbian for this one, but severe stomach pain is keeping him on the sidelines after missing the Ferencvaros game too. Considering that Matteo Gabbia and Fikayo Tomori were already on the injury list, it’s certainly not ideal.

Mike Maignan will be back between the sticks, at least, after serving a suspension in the Europa League. He will have a defence of Mario Gila, Koni De Winter, Sankhoun Diawara and Pervis Estupinan ahead of him, with the left flank being the main worry there.

In midfield, Ardon Jashari will be demoted to the bench as Luka Modric will be back from start, partnering with Yunus Musah in a tandem. Adrien Rabiot, meanwhile, is expected to have a slightly more advanced role.

Diego Moreira and Christian Pulisic will be the danger men on the attacking flanks, looking to replicate their success against Inter in the derby. The same cannot be said for Goncalo Ramos, who instead needs to get going with his goalscoring after bagging just two league goals so far.

In terms of firepower on the bench, Amorim could turn to Davide Bartesaghi, the aforementioned Jashari, Omari Hutchinson and Alphadjo Cisse. Francesco Camarda will also be there, of course, but whether he can be categorised as an impact player is a different question.

Predicted Milan XI (4-2-3-1): Maignan; Gila, De Winter, Diawara, Estupinan; Musah, Modric; Moreira, Rabiot, Pulisic; Ramos.

Predicted bench: Torriani, Bouyer, F. Terracciano, Vladimirov, Bartesaghi, Jashari, Comotto, Hutchinson, Cisse, Camarda.

Injuries & suspensions: Chukwueze, Gabbia, Loftus-Cheek, Pavlovic, Saelemaekers, Terracciano, Tomori.

After what was a disastrous start to the 2025-26 season under Patrick Vieira, sitting rock bottom between the 7th and 9th matchdays, Daniele De Rossi was appointed. He quickly brought them above the relegation zone and managed to keep them there, finishing seven points clear in the end.

It was only right to confirm him for this season, as such, and there have been no regrets so far. The start was admittedly dodgy, with one draw and four defeats in the first five games, but there have been four wins and one defeat in the five games since. Crisis very much averted.

The only defeat in the last five games came against De Rossi’s former club as well, namely Roma, who have been on fire at the start of this season. There can be no accusations of the former midfielder throwing the game to benefit his former club, let’s put it that way.

Nevertheless, the poor star has been cancelled out and it’s possible that De Rossoneri just needed some time, especially after losing key players like Jeff Ekhator (to Juventus) and Brooke Norton-Vuffy (to Hull City). The Serie A veteran Ruslan Malinovskyi also waved goodbye as his contract expired.

On the flip side, the Rossoblu did keep hold of most of the key players from last season, such as Johan Vasquez, Leo Ostigard, Morten Frendrup, and Lorenzo Colombo. The latter was signed permanently from Milan after a season-long loan, as the conditions for the obligation were triggered.

Some interesting signings were also made, from the veteran (and former Milan man) Stephan El Shaarawy to the familiar face Hamed Traore, who played for Sassuolo between 2019 and 2013. After spells at Bournemouth, Napoli, Auxerre and Marseille, he’s now looking to regain stability.

As for the latest team news, De Rossi has a much better situation than Amorim with no injuries or suspensions to note. Lorenzo Venturino and Elias Havel were both on the sidelines but have returned for this one.

It looks like the former Milan men El Shaarawy and Colombo will start, while Junior Messias could perhaps be introduced from the bench. De Rossi didn’t give much away during his presser in terms of the team selection, but he did make it clear that Genoa cannot get too cocky.

Genoa (3-5-2): Bijlow; Marcandalli, Ostigard, Vasquez; Ellertsson, Baldanzi, Frendrup, Traore, El Shaarawy; Colombo, Osmajic.

Predicted bench: Stolz, Sommariva, Messias, Otoa, Sabelli, Puczka, Amorim, Meichtry, Mitaj, Vitinha, Sow, Drameh, Ehizibue, Wiafe, Venturino, Havel.

This will be the 157th encounter between the two sides, with Milan looking to build on their 71 wins in the fixture, while Genoa are quite far behind at 37 wins. 48 games have ended all square, which is a tally that has increased lately.

In the last five games, there have been three draws (3-3, 0-0 and 1-1), but the good news for the Rossoneri is that they won the other two games. The five games before that were all wins as well, with no defeat recorded against the Rossoblu since 2020.

The most recent win was a 2-1 win at the Marassi in favour of Milan, courtesy of goals from Christopher Nkunku and Zachary Athekame. They both left the club on loan this summer, so they won’t be of any help tomorrow.

In the game before that, Rafael Leao (another player who’s no longer in the team) rescued a point in the dying minutes after Colombo had given Genoa the lead in the first half.

➤ Milan are unbeaten in their last 10 Serie A matches against Genoa (W6 D4). Since 2020-21, the Rossoneri only have a longer run of unbeaten games against Hellas Verona (12).

➤ Milan have won each of their last four Serie A away games against Genoa; only against three teams do the Rossoneri currently have a longer running away win streak in the competition: Hellas Verona (seven), Messina (five) and Pisa (five).

➤ Genoa are on a three-game winning streak in Serie A. The last time the Rossoblu recorded a streak of four consecutive wins in a single season in the top flight was between March and April 2009.

➤ Genoa have won three of their first six home games this season (D1 L2). The Rossoblu haven’t won four of their first seven Serie A home games since the 2015-16 season, when Gian Piero Gasperini was in charge.

➤ Ruben Amorim’s Milan have scored 21 goals in their first 10 Serie A games this season. The last Rossoneri manager to do better after his first 11 league games was Cesare Maldini in 1972, with 25 goals.

➤ Milan have won two and drawn three of their first five away games this season. The Rossoneri have avoided defeat in their first six away games of the season on three occasions between 2020-21 and 2025-26, the same number as Inter and Napoli, and could now become the only team to do so four times in this span, including the current season.

➤ Genoa have scored four goals in the first 15 minutes of Serie A action this season, a figure they share with Frosinone, Atalanta, Lazio, and Roma and second only to Milan (five). However, the Rossoblu are the only team yet to score in the final 15 minutes of a match.

➤ Stephan El Shaarawy, who scored 21 goals in 83 league appearances for AC Milan, has provided an assist in each of his last two Serie A appearances. Should he also contribute to a goal in his next match, he would become, at 34 years and 12 days, the second-oldest player to achieve this feat in three consecutive games for Genoa since 2004-05, after Domenico Criscito in 2021 (34 years and 269 days).

➤ Adrien Rabiot has bagged five goals and three assists in his first eight league appearances this season. The last midfielder to do better in the first nine games of a Serie A season was Sergej Milinkovic-Savic in 2022-23, with 10 contributions (three goals and seven assists).

Furthermore, the Frenchman has been involved in a goal in each of his last five appearances, and the last Milan midfielder to do better in Serie A was Kaka in 2004 (seven).

➤ Diego Moreira has been directly involved in seven goals (four goals and three assists) in his first six Serie A appearances for AC Milan. Since 2004/05, no player has had more in his first six league games for the Rossoneri, equaling Mario Balotelli’s record (seven in 2013).

Starting with Milan, and at the back, I think we should keep an extra eye on Diawara. He did well in the derby but after switching to centre-back for the Ferencvaros game, his display just wasn’t good enough. He will have the same position tomorrow and we need to see an improvement.

Further up the pitch, Moreira showed in the derby that he can be just the X-factor that Milan need, and this game should be an even better one for him (at least on paper). The cameo against Ferencvaros was positive despite the result, so hopefully he can build on the last week.

As for the home side, I’m curious to see Traore in action again. He definitely had some very good games for Sassuolo against the Rossoneri and with more consistent playing time at Genoa, he could rediscover that spark which made him so special.

Finally, I think it would be silly to ignore the defensive general, Vasquez. He always seems to have good games against Milan and honestly, he’s the type of player the Rossoneri could use right now, at least as a deputy.

It’s been incredibly hard to predict how well Milan will do so far this season, with many ups and downs. However, my gut feeling tells me that they will be able to do a whole lot better than on Thursday, especially since the key players are back from start.

My only worry is the left-hand side of the defence, with Diawara and Estupinan set to start there. As such, I’m going to go for a narrow win, with no clean sheet either. Let us know your prediction in the comments below!

Oscar Short Film Race Spotlights Bold Stories, New Animation Tech and Creative Risk-Taking

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Some of the most compelling stories in contention for the Academy Awards aren’t unfolding across two or three hours. They’re being told in a fraction of that time. The race in the short film categories is taking shape, offering a showcase for emerging filmmakers, well-known talents and storytellers pushing the boundaries of what can be accomplished in 40 minutes or less.Spanning live action, animation and documentary filmmaking, the short film categories have long served as a launching pad for directors and a creative outlet for industry veterans eager to experiment outside the constraints of feature-length filmmaking.Short films often operate with modest budgets and limited — if any — distribution. But those constraints can also encourage creative risk-taking, resulting in intimate character studies, groundbreaking animation and documentaries that tackle volatile subjects with remarkable economy.Anne Sundberg, helmer of “A Month in Minnesota,” a documentary that chronicles the fallout from the fatal ICE shootings in Minneapolis, wanted her film to show the community response to this violence.“The first thing was I really wanted to bring people inside the experience of what it was to be in Minnesota at the time that this happened,” says Sundberg. “There were networks that developed. There was a clear sense of purpose. This could have been any city in the U.S. and it happened in Minneapolis, and it was a normal city where kids went to the park and played, where people felt safe going to school, where people generally felt pretty safe going to work, and it turned in a moment. And I think what you saw happen in Minnesota was that people learned quickly how to form networks how to trust each other, how to figure out who needed what.”Short documentaries can also zoom in on one person and their unique struggles, as in “Listen,” directed by Taliesin Black-Brown, who followed sound recordist Hank Lentfer as he documented sounds in nature as he cares for his mother, who’s in the final stages of dementia.“It’s my real hope that it just inspires people to listen, to maybe pause and then to kind of just experience nature and the sounds around them,” says Black-Brown. “It can be profound to experience those sounds and I think sometimes there’s so much noise in our environment, whether it’s the noise in urban places, or someone is doing construction over there. Just to make space to sit in a park and listen to a bird. It can be really healing.”Some of this year’s early frontrunners in the animated shorts category come from the singular visions of directors committed to a genre or experimenting with new technologies. Short films are often a place for new animation techniques to be given an opportunity to be used in surprising ways.For the director of “Praying Mantis,” it was a way to bring his love of hand-drawn 2D animation together with his passion for horror films. Helmer Joe Hsieh spent about four years bringing this project to the screen.“I think short films are a very, very good way to show that horror and animation are full of imagination,” says Hsieh, who is based in Taiwan. “A short film is also a good way to show that you can make what you imagined into something. You start with the idea of the character and you build from there to make a story for the audiences.”With “Dear Upstairs Neighbors,” the filmmakers wanted to push emerging AI technologies to take artist-created drawings and assemble a short based on those drawings while guided by the artists. The movie follows a frazzled insomniac as she tries to get some sleep.Marcia Mayer, executive producer of the short who formerly worked at Pixar, sees the controversy around AI as similar to what happened when CG tech came along and disrupted the hand-drawn pipeline.“Short films are often done as a test of new technology in animation,” says Mayer. “We scanned 35 paintings and trained a model on the different styles of painting. We’re using AI to create the look the filmmakers always wanted and it’s making it possible to make this film in a realistic timeline. It became possible for Connie [He], the director, to use these tools to make the film she wanted to make.”In the live action category, the landscape is also diverse, with filmmakers sometimes taking inspiration from their own lives or even coming to subjects after they’re commissioned to create a short.“Jozefa,” helmed by Kathryn Kalucki, was crafted as a short after the filmmakers had looked for a way to create a story about the director’s grandmother and her early experiences as a refugee.“My producing partner and I decided we had to use the resources we have, the talent we have and the time we have to create a short,” says Kalucki. “We shot for a very feverish two-and-a-half days, and we had a one-week prep. We shot in Burbank and we were able to make something powerful, even with all those limitations.”Filmmakers who create short films are often pushing against limited resources and abbreviated shooting schedules, but for creators like Patrick Willems, who directed the live action short “Power Trip,” that’s not new. It may actually even be a bit easier to work with a crew who are used to moving quickly to capture what they need over just a few days.“I make YouTube videos for a living, and I have a channel where I do long-form film criticism, but I also weave in narrative filmmaking into everything,” says Willems. “I’m used to having almost no resources and having to do everything myself. So making a short like this feels amazing because I don’t have to do everything myself and there’s a great professional crew working with me. You learn there’s no time to waste.”Mekhai Lee, who directed the live action short “Them That’s Not,” also sees the short as a unique art form and not necessarily a bid to a feature. There are advantages for him to tell his stories in this way.“It’s like the novella to the novel,” says Lee. “I like the short story. I love reading short stories by [James] Baldwin. I love Octavia Butler. I just finished a series of short stories by her. And these are novelists. The short films just work in a different way. There’s a different approach to it. I think the way that this film was executed in this time, in this space, within these parameters, that is exactly what and how it needed to be done. This is exactly the best way to put the story into the world.”Seasoned filmmakers like Academy Award-winning Sean Baker (“Anora”) find making a short an opportunity to explore themes and develop techniques that have interested them. His live action short, “Sandiwara,” stars Oscar-winner Michelle Yeoh, and is the fruit of fashion house Self-Portrait’s residency program.“I think you have to always be practicing your craft and learning new things,” says Baker. “I never consider myself an expert filmmaker. So, when these opportunities come around and I connect with the idea, it’s just an opportunity for further learning and it was wonderful to connect with a new crew that worked on this short.”Filmmaker Maggie Gyllenhaal has a couple of features and a lot of acclaim on her resumé, and views shorts as a type of art that gives a voice to projects not always meant to be developed as features. Her live action short, “Flesh Impact,” focuses on the life of Marilyn Monroe and stars Ellen Burstyn and Dakota Johnson.“I think some stories are meant to be 17 minutes, like mine,” says Gyllenhaal, who was commissioned by Genesis to create a short film about Monroe. “Short films don’t get distribution. It’s not realistic to finance something like this. I think it’s an interesting medium that isn’t totally rooted in commercialism.But where does this live? I think of short films almost like these little geodes that look like a rock from the outside but when you turn it over it’s full of something unexplainable.” Related Stories 'Everyone in My Family Has Killed Someone' Series Adds Eight, Including Sean Astin, Patricia Clarkson Chris Pratt to Continue 'Terminal List' Story in 'Savage Son' Series for Amazon

Alanis Morissette Comes Out as Pansexual

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Alanis Morissette—who shares kids Ever, 14, Onyx, 9, and Winter, 6, with husband Mario "Souleye" Treadway—shared that she identifies as pansexual: “I like to leave it for the epilogue.”

Alanis Morissette thinks you oughta know a bit more about her personal life.

After Fortune Feimster asked the Grammy winner if the men who inspired her breakup songs ever made her think about swinging a different way, Morissette dispelled precious illusions about her sexuality, sharing she identifies as pansexual.

“I’m sorry for the men that hurt you, but I’m not sorry for the songs that came from it,” Feimster told Morissette on a recent episode of her, Tig Notaro and Mae Martin’s Handsome Podcast. “Did it make you want to be a lesbian at any point?”

As the “You Oughta Know” singer replied, “I’m pansexual, so, yeah.”

Surprised by Morissette’s admission that she’s attracted to all sexes, Notaro chimed in “Wait, you’re not really pansexual, are you?” to which the “Ironic” singer confirmed, “I am.”

When Notaro joked that the “You Learn” singer should have started the podcast with the confession, Morissette joked, “I like to leave it for the epilogue.”

“I like to confuse people on the way out,” she quipped. “Is it confusing?”

But it wouldn’t be the only time the 52-year-old—who shares kids Ever, 14, Onyx, 9, and Winter, 6, with husband Mario "Souleye" Treadway—has pulled her hand out of her pocket when it comes to her private life.

After all, Morissette made a sweet confession about the fulfillment motherhood has given her while getting inducted into the Songwriters Hall of Fame, sharing the role is by far her most prestigious honor.

“Archetypically, ‘Mom’ is number one for me,” she told E! News ahead of the ceremony at the Marriott Maquis in New York June 11, “and ‘writer’ is number two, so for that to be marked tonight, it really warms my heart.”

And while Morissette considers family her crowning achievement, she also shared how much it meant to receive acclaim after inspiring a new generation of writers.

“Any of us writers, women, non-gendered, binary identifying, men frankly,” she continued. “There’s a lot of men who I connect with on this level. I think it’s those of us who are very sensitive and those of us who are empaths, we unite in a very quiet introverted way, and a lot of writers are introverts.”

But Morissette has a lot to say “thank U” for as of late. After all, she just began her Inglewood residency Butterfly With a Machete Nov. 5.

“The ‘90s message for me was basically stay in one lane as though that’s even possible for a whole human being,” she explained. “So this musical show, piece—I don’t even know what to call it. It’s a trauma-edy.”

As she noted, “It’s taking all the challenges and some of the stories of my life and transmuting it into dance or movement or stories, comedy—and always music. Music’s number one.”

But Morissette isn’t the only star to open up about her sexuality. Keep reading for more stars who have done the same...

The Dance Moms alum didn't give it a second thought when she came out in a 2021 Instagram story.

"I came out when I was 17 but I didn't understand at the time what a big deal that was," she told People in October 2023. "I didn't understand why that was scary."

"Of course there was a lot of positivity and love and acceptance but then there was a lot of negativity, and I did lose a lot," she continued. "I didn't care, and I still don't care [about] the things I lost and the people that I lost and if I lost a chunk of fans...because it's who I am."

While the "Flowers" singer was never a big fan of labels, she came out as pansexual in 2016.

"My whole life, I didn't understand my own gender and my own sexuality," she revealed to Variety in October 2016. "I always hated the word 'bisexual,' because that's even putting me in a box. I don't ever think about someone being a boy or someone being a girl."

She continued, "Also, my nipple pasties and s--t never felt sexualized to me. My eyes started opening in the fifth or sixth grade. My first relationship in my life was with a chick."

For Matt, coming out to his conservative Christian family marked the start of a new era in his life.

"Telling your family is a huge, huge deal," the White Collar alum, who shares three kids with husband Simon Halls, previously told OUT Magazine. "I really view my life as divided between the time before I told my parents, and the time after. And the decisions I made, and the life I lived, before and after, are vastly different. It's night and day."

The "Good Luck, Babe!" singer revealed that she isn't always as confident and comfortable as she may seem. And her Midwest roots played a huge role.

"I was scared of flamboyantly gay people because I was taught that," she told Rolling Stone. "[I realized] people hate flamboyantly gay people because it just exudes femininity, and people hate women. Just little things like that, you're like, 'Oh my God, this is just so f--ked.'"

"It took a lot of unlearning, and there's still things I"m still confused about, and [it's] why I feel so uncomfortable being gay sometimes," she continued. "I don't get why this is such an issue for me. It shouldn't be, but something's just going on and I need to just accept that."

The Bachelorette alum came out on an episode of The View, when she revealed she was in a committed relationship with Robby Hoffman, the comedian she wed in January 2025.

"I've been keeping it kind of private," she said in the August 2023 episode, "because it is a bigger story and a bigger conversation because I'm dating a girl."

"I think it was always a whisper in me that just got louder and louder," she continued. "I didn't really know to pay attention to it. When this happens there's some shame attached to it and I had to a little bit navigate through that shame; what is it, where is it coming from."

The Mean Girls star, having previously identified as bisexual, came out as lesbian in January 2024—on an episode of Saturday Night Live. And her friends played a supporting role in the decision.

“All of a sudden, I had these people around me who were either nonbinary people, trans friends, or lesbians," she told Billboard months later, "and I was like, ‘I feel so happy when I’m with you guys. And I also really want to call myself a lesbian when I’m around you.’”

The former late-night host officially came out as queer on the last day of Pride month in 2024.

“In what will come as a shock to exactly zero people, I’m using the last day of PRIDE to come out!” she wrote in a June 2024 Instagram post, wearing a tank with the word “Queer” printed on the front.

“Be proud of who you are, little babies! I know I am!” she continued. “And I can’t wait to be discriminated against for a new reason!!”

Before revealing her sexuality, the former Disney Channel star was actually nervous to publicly come out as queer.

"I've hinted about my sexuality for years while being afraid to spell it out for everybody," she told Gay Times in May 2021. "I did a lyric video last year, for 'We Belong.' It had moving line drawings of people falling in love…It was all a man and a woman making out and it was a weird moment for me."

"When the song came out, everybody got the idea that the song was a big LGBTQ+ anthem song and I found myself in this position where everyone thought I was queerbaiting," she continued. "I went on Instagram Live and said 'Guys, I really needed to explain something to you. Maybe I haven't said it, but I'm super queer. This is something I want to represent through my music because it's who I am.'"

The "Heart Attack" singer came out as nonbinary in a 2021 Instagram post.

"Every day we wake up, we are given another opportunity and chance to be who we want and wish to be," she wrote in the caption. "I've spent the majority of my life growing in front of all of you… you've seen the good, the bad, and everything in between. Not only has my life been a journey for myself, I was also living for those on the other side of the cameras."

"Today is a day I'm so happy to share more of my life with you all - I am proud to let you know that I identify as non-binary and will officially be changing my pronouns to they/them moving forward," Demi—who has since gone back to using she/her pronouns—continued. "This has come after a lot of healing and self-reflective work. I'm still learning and coming into myself, and I don't claim to be an expert or a spokesperson. Sharing this with you now opens another level of vulnerability for me."

Though the Bachelor alum has been dating singer Hayley Kiyoko since 2018, she did not publicly come out until four years into her relationship. And though she admitted it felt weird once everyone found out, she saw the impact of publicly sharing her truth.

"I realized this is so much bigger than me and my relationship and my personal experience because if it helped anyone feel comfortable or brave to be comfortable with who they fall in love with, then that's way bigger than me and my fears and what I was scared of," she told E! News in June 2022. "It's very humbling to read comments like that and realize the significance of something as simple as falling in love and sharing that."

Three years after coming out to his family at age 15, Troye publicly shared his story in a YouTube video. And as he later recalled, his decision to come out occurred amid a pivotal moment in his life.

"I was about to sign my record deal, and I really wanted to be in charge of how I came out," he told People in February 2022. "I didn't want anyone to take that away from me. I wanted to start going out and going to gay clubs and meeting boys, and I wanted to write songs about love that were true and genuine."

The Selling Sunset star—who is married to singer G Flip—stresses the importance of prioritizing yourself.

"If someone truly loves you, your happiness should always come before their beliefs," Chrishell, who began her romance with the Australian singer in 2022, told E! News. "Never conform to others ideas they have for your life. It's yours and you only get one."

The TikToker—who was openly gay at an all-boys high school—uses his platform to encourage others to be comfortable with who they are.

"My identity will already be something that can be of controversy, depending on who the audience is," Chris told E! News in October 2022. "So one of my passive-active missions in having this platform is to try to break that down and bring normalcy to this identity."

The Bravo star, who came out while in college, shared some valuable advice for having those big coming out conversations.

"My advice would be, find one friend that you can tell," he previously told E! News. "All it takes is one friend that you know will be supportive. And that friend can be your island and just kind of branch out from there. It's always worse in your head than it's going to be."

Tottenham to submit formal complaint over Jayden Bogle decision

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Bogle landed on Mathys Tel’s ankle during a challenge but VAR decided not to overturn the on-field decision and issue the Leeds defender a red card

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Tottenham Hotspur will submit a formal complaint to Pro Ref over the decision not to dismiss defender Jayden Bogle during the 1-0 defeat to Leeds at Elland Road.

It is understood Tottenham believe Bogle should have been sent off and will write to refereeing body Pro Ref to seek an explanation as to why the incident did not result in a red card.

Bogle lunged into a tackle on Spurs forward Mathys Tel and landed on Tel’s ankle with his left foot. The on-field decision from referee John Brooks was to award a foul to Spurs but issue no cards of further punishment to Bogle.

The game was paused as Tel received treatment on the pitch and the Video Assistant Referee looked in detail at the incident. The decision from the VAR arrived after a four-minte review with Tony Harrington opting against calling Brooks to the pitch-side monitor to review the challenge himself.

The challenge was deemed “reckless” but the match continued with Bogle left unpunished and Tel eventually forced off due to lingering affects on his Achilles.

The incident came just a week after Crystal Palace's Jean-Philippe Mateta was sent off for a similar challenge in Spurs' win over the Eagles. Later in the game, Tottenham’s Pedro Porro wa shown a yellow card for a challenge similar to Bogle’s which infuriated manager Roberto de Zerbi.

"I have big confidence in the referee. Nothing changes if he goes to the monitor," said De Zerbi. "If they didn't take the right decision I don't know. We have to keep the eyes on the performance where can improve.

"I was surprised on the yellow card for Pedro Porro because it was clear. The potential red card, I couldn't say. We have full confidence in the referee and VAR. I cannot say nothing more."

Three things we learnt from Bundesliga matchday 9 | Felix Nmecha, RB Leipzig find stability and will there actually be a title race?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Bundesliga matchday nine began with a game between two strugglers in Hamburg and Borussia Mönchengladbach, with Merlin Polzin’s side coming out 3-1 winners at the Volksparkstadion.

Saturday afternoon saw eight teams in action. After last weekend’s 2-2 draw in ‘Der Klassiker’, Borussia Dortmund hosted high-flyers Elversberg, while Bayern Munich travelled to Mainz. Also, Paderborn played Eintracht Frankfurt, and RB Leipzig faced Augsburg.

It is safe to say Felix Nmecha’s start at Dortmund was a very slow one, so much so that he was venturing into flop territory, but he has been able to turn his career around, and it is safe to say that this season, the 26-year-old is one of Dortmund’s most consistent, and maybe even best, players.

Nmecha’s brace against Elversberg helped Niko Kovac’s side maintain their unbeaten Bundesliga record, as well as keep pace with Bayern Munich at the top of the table.

This week as a whole can also sum up Nmecha’s season so far, as he put in a strong performance midweek in a tough 2-1 win against Real Betis in the Champions League.

Obviously, Nmecha is not the only Dortmund midfielder finding their form this season, with Jobe Bellingham also playing at a much higher level and even earning a call-up to the England senior squad. Summer signing Joey Veerman is slowly establishing himself in the team as Nmecha’s midfield partner.

There have been, and continue to be, question marks about Martin Demichelis as a head coach and whether he is fit for the job at RB Leipzig, but in the last few games they have found stability under the Argentine.

Heading into this game, in their last four games, they won three and lost once, which was a commendable performance against Manchester City in the Champions League.

While today, despite going 1-0 down within the first minute, Leipzig managed to rally and went in at half-time 3-1 up thanks to goals from Ezechiel Banzuzi, Christopher Nkunku and David Raum. Then after the break, Romulo and Christopher Baumgartner also scored, while a late Augsburg goal made it 5-2.

Obviously, there are still rumours in the media about Julian Nagelsmann’s name being an option at Leipzig again, but these run of results have more than likely relieved some of the pressure on Demichelis. Especially as after the international break, Leipzig have a tough run of Bundesliga fixtures.

We are now just over 25% of the way through this Bundesliga season, and both Dortmund and Bayern are currently neck and neck, with only the reigning champions’ superior goal difference the reason for them leading the table, and today they matched each others results again with Bayern beating Mainz 2-1 while Dortmund beat Elversberg.

At this stage last season, Dortmund were seven points behind Bayern in third, while second placed Leipzig were five points behind, so the fact that Dortmund are currently level with the Bundesliga rekordmeister is a good indication of the gap they have closed on Bayern.

While this season, something seems off with Vincent Kompany’s side in the league. Even against Mainz, the result wasn’t as comfortable as they would of liked and then there have been draws with Augsburg, Schalke and Dortmund this season too. Granted, Bayern have been very unlucky in front of goal in those games, but they have not been as ruthless as last season, for example.

The only potentially stumbling block on whether there will be a Bundesliga title race this season, will be Dortmund, and whether they can maintain their form, despite so many of their games being close affairs.

Conventional Vs. Transit Design: Why Some School Buses Have Flat Fronts

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

School buses haven't changed much since 1939, but they came a long way before then. Some of the first vehicles used to take kids to school were simply horse-drawn wagons. It wasn't until the start of World War I that the first motorized buses seem to have appeared in this country. That's when a company called Wayne Works introduced a model in 1914. It was built on a long-wheelbase Model T chassis, so its engine was in the traditional location, under the hood up front.

Fast-forward a few years and we find Crown Coach evolving the concept of motorized school buses even further. Crown was a major commercial-vehicle manufacturer, founded in 1903 to sell business wagons to businessmen. It would survive all the way up to 1990 by building not only buses, but also hearses, fire trucks, bakery trucks, ambulances, and even airplanes.

Crown brought some notable innovations to the bus field as well. For example, it introduced the forward-control school bus in 1932. This wasn't the first forward-control bus of any type in the country, though. A firm called Twin Coach has that honor. The company had already been building flat-faced delivery trucks, and during the Depression, it brought the layout to its small buses — including some meant for municipal transit.

The head start for flat-faced city buses meant that when flat-faced school buses became popular, folks looked back to the first application to name the second. The new wave of so-called transit-style school buses gave school districts a new way to transport kids, with both new advantages and new drawbacks compared to conventional school buses.

First off, let's deal with their industry classifications: Class C buses are conventional buses, with engines in front and the main exit/entrance located behind the front wheels. The flat-front buses are in Class D and can theoretically have their engines anywhere from beside the driver to the very back of the bus. In the real world, positioning the motor at the rear is a big-time benefit to driver and students alike. That way, they face less noise, heat, and air pollution.

The transit-style layout also improves the driver's vision, since there's no massive hood to see over and around. Conventional buses are more like those big pickups with huge frontal blind spots that just keep getting larger as the years go by. Additionally, the flat-face setup maximizes how many passengers can fit in a given footprint. The difference isn't huge, but the flat-face buses can generally fit up to 90 passengers while conventional versions max out at about 77 to 83.

For their part, the traditional buses tend to be more maneuverable than their alternatives. This is partly due to their different wheelbases and how they can change a vehicle's handling. Looking at data from the Bureau of Transportation, Class C conventional buses with a recommended wheelbase of about 21.3 feet have noticeably shorter turning radii than Class D transit-style models, with recommended wheelbases of 23 feet. Lower maintenance costs are another strong suit of the conventional buses, as it's much easier to access their engines.

In the end, the reason you'll see conventional buses in some places and transit-style versions elsewhere is a matter of local preference. But neither class is likely to save kids from traumatic school bus stories.

JPMorgan and Mastercard back Seattle startup Firmly’s bet on AI shopping agents

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

by Todd Bishop on Nov 7, 2026 at 10:23 amNovember 7, 2026 at 10:32 am

Firmly, a Seattle-area startup that helps online retailers sell through AI shopping assistants, raised a $15 million Series A round led by Squarepoint Capital, with participation from J.P. Morgan Payments, Mastercard, Samsung Next and others.

The company, founded in 2019, connects retailers’ existing online stores to AI assistants, marketplaces and other shopping channels, so people can buy products inside those third-party apps and services without going to retailers’ websites. Retailers keep their own payment providers and customer relationships.

“Every new AI assistant, marketplace or shopping platform is another place to sell. But connecting to each one can take serious engineering, because catalogs, inventory, payments, and checkout all have to work together,” wrote Kumar Senthil, Firmly’s co-founder and CEO, in a blog post announcing the round.

Mastercard is a commercial partner as well as an investor. Firmly handles the merchant side of Mastercard Agent Connect, a service launched in September to link merchants, AI agents and payment providers through a single integration.

Senthil told Axios Pro that Mastercard is recruiting merchants for the service, giving Firmly a route to new customers.

Firmly charges merchants $2,000 to $15,000 a month, depending on the total value of merchandise they sell, Senthil told Axios. He said connecting a merchant took four to six weeks in 2024 and now takes a day or less, including testing.

The company lists Best Buy, Furniture.com and Backcountry among its retail customers. RetailMeNot and an AI shopping app called Wizard also use its technology.

The funding comes as AI shopping agents move into the mainstream. Meta’s Muse assistant can browse retail sites and buy items with a user’s approval. Amazon has blocked Muse from its site and is suing Perplexity over a shopping agent in the startup’s Comet browser.

Firmly takes a different approach from those agents. Instead of having software click through a retailer’s web pages, it connects directly to the retailer’s systems with its permission.

The company this week also launched Ask Agent, an AI shopping assistant for retailers’ own websites. Shoppers describe what they want, get product recommendations and can check out without leaving the chat.

Other investors in the round include Alumni Ventures, ARK Invest, Benhamou Global Ventures, Granada Funds, Hike Ventures, The Mercantile Companies and Versus Ventures.

The round brings Firmly’s total funding to about $20 million. The company had raised $5.2 million when it partnered with Perplexity in March 2025 on shopping features inside the AI search app.

Senthil spent eight years at Microsoft and later led e-commerce products at Samsung. He co-founded Firmly with Damien Balsan, a co-founder of LoopPay, the mobile payments startup acquired by Samsung, and Murali Krishnamurthy, Firmly’s CTO and a former chief technical architect for Samsung’s U.S. and global e-commerce business.

Skip The Oil And Eggs: Add A Can Of Diet Coke To This Cake Mix For The Easiest Dessert

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

Boxed cake mix is hard to beat when you need a dessert in a hurry, or if you just don't back your baking abilities. One of our favorites is Duncan Hines Devil's Food Cake, available on Amazon, which came in second in our boxed cake mix ranking. Like most packaged options, it comes with all the dry ingredients pre-measured, leaving you to add water, eggs, and vegetable oil. However, just because the instructions are printed on the back doesn't mean you have to follow them to the letter. In fact, you can swap all three for a can of Diet Coke and still come out with a soft, fluffy cake.

To understand why this works, it helps to know what eggs and oil actually do. Egg whites provide proteins that coagulate as the cake bakes, forming a structure that holds the batter together and helps trap the air responsible for its fluffy crumb. The fat from oil and egg yolks coats the flour particles and inhibits gluten development, helping produce a softer, more tender texture. Diet Coke also contributes to the cake's crumb and tenderness, but in a very different way.

Diet Coke's secret weapon is its carbonation. It contains dissolved carbon dioxide, which forms tiny bubbles when added to the batter. As the cake heats up in the oven, these bubbles expand and help the mixture rise. The soda's acidity can also react with leavening agents present in the mix, such as baking soda, further contributing to the cake's light texture. To top things off, a standard 12-ounce can of Diet Coke contains roughly the same volume as the combined water and oil that Duncan Hines' mix calls for, making it a super convenient add-in. If you don't have Diet Coke on hand, Coke Zero is a near-identical substitute.

To get the most out of this swap, start with a can of cold Diet Coke over one that's been sitting at room temperature. Ever noticed how a can of cold Diet Coke has that unbeatable crispness? That's because carbon dioxide prefers to hang around in cold liquids over warm ones. Once a soda can is cracked open, it releases gas, so a cold one retains its carbonation better as you prepare your batter. On that note, avoid over-mixing the batter as this can knock out the trapped gas. If you notice bubbling, that's a telling sign you need to slow down.

When swapping oil and eggs for Diet Coke, there's one important difference worth noting. Diet Coke famously contains no fat, so while it can help the cake rise, it can't replicate the richness that oil brings to the table. It's a great substitution if you're looking to make a lighter, less calorie-dense cake. But for something that'll produce a richer cake with all the same effects as Diet Coke, reach for good old full-sugar Coke instead.

Diet Coke gets its sweetness from aspartame, an artificial sweetener, which loses its sweetness when exposed to heat. Regular Coke opts for high-fructose corn syrup, which is essentially liquid sugar. Unlike aspartame, high fructose corn syrup thrives under heat, and is already a baking superstar. In the oven, its resident sugars caramelize to give the cake a richer flavor, gooier texture, and a beautifully browned color. In fact, any soda will work wonders for boxed cake mix; for a similarly rich flavor profile with a cherry backbone, pick up a can of Cherry Coke or Dr Pepper, or for something brighter, go for Sprite or 7UP.

Passenger Cut Off From Alcohol Throws Suitcase Off Plane Stairs—Gets 7 Days In Jail

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

An Uzbekistan Airways passenger demanded more alcohol, cursed at the crew after being cut off, and kept the tantrum going upon landing. Then he threw his own suitcase off the aircraft stairs and kicked it across the ramp. The next day he received a seven day sentence. The 36-year-old Russian man was traveling on flight HY552 from Kuala Lumpur to Tashkent on November 5. According to local reporting on the court proceedings, the senior flight attendant refused his repeated requests for alcohol because he was already intoxicated. He responded by swearing at her and other passengers. Another flight attendant tried to calm him down and received more abuse. Police boarded on arrival in Tashkent. They told him to calm down, leave the aircraft, and come with them to the airport’s administrative building to explain himself. He refused to cooperate and continued swearing. Video of his exit< shows him throwing the suitcase over the side of the stairs. It lands on the pavement with its handle extended. He walks down, approaches the bag, and kicks it away. Usually when a suitcase gets treated like this, the passenger files a claim with the airline.
